For I swear in Good Faith that I have uncovered the Truth. If you work in the Mercury I have described and the most pure **Common Gold** with the proper Fire, you will find **Our Sun** within seven or nine months at most, and **Our Moon** within five months. And these are the true time limits for completing these Sulfurs; if you then believe them to be our Stones, you would still be in error. But from these, through repeated Labor with at least a sensible Fire, you will have the true Elixir, and all this within a Year and a Half, God willing, to whom be Glory forever.

Furthermore, there is another Way which is opened to the Upright, the Holy, the Humble, and those who fear God, who seek not only their own utility but that of All. In this Operation, once the Mercury is prepared, the **Fixation** into **Our Sun** <note>original: "Solem Nostrum," referring to Philosophical Gold</note> occurs within eight Days.

### CHAPTER XX.
### On the Arrival of Blackness in the Work of the Sun and Moon.

If you have worked in the **Sun** and **Moon** <note>Sun and Moon: Alchemical code for Gold and Silver, or the masculine and feminine principles of the Work</note> so that you might seek **Our Sulphur** in them, consider whether you have seen your Matter swollen like Paste, or

boiling like Water, or rather like liquid Pitch. This is because **Our Gold** and **Our Mercury** have an emblematic type in the Work of **Common Gold** <note>original: "Solis Vulgi"</note> with **Our Mercury**. Once the Furnace is lit, wait in the boiling Heat for twenty Days, during which Time you will observe Various Colors. But around the end of the fourth week, provided the Heat has been continuous, you will see a lovely **Greenness** <note>original: "Viredinem"</note>, which will not disappear for ten days or thereabouts. Rejoice then, because for certain you will shortly see the Whole as Black as Coal, and all the Members of your Compound will be reduced to Atoms. 

For this Operation is nothing other than the **Resolution of the Fixed into the Non-Fixed** <term>Resolution: The process of breaking down a solid substance into its constituent parts or a liquid state</term>, so that both, being joined afterward, may form One Matter that is partly Spiritual and partly Corporeal. Therefore the Philosopher says: "Take the **Corascene Dog** and the **Armenian Bitch**, join them together, and they will give birth to a Son of the color of the Heavens." <note>The "Corascene Dog" and "Armenian Bitch" are famous metaphors in alchemy, likely representing metallic sulfur and mercurial water which "die" to produce a new substance.</note> For these Natures, by a brief boiling <note>original: "Decoctione"</note>, will be turned into a Broth like the Foam of the Sea, or a thick Cloud, which will be tinged with a leaden Color. And I swear to you in good faith that I have hidden nothing except the **Regimen** <term>Regimen: The "governance" or management of the heat and timing, considered the most secret part of alchemy</term>; yet this, if you are prudent, you will very easily gather from my words. Once the Regimen is known, take up the Stone demonstrated above—

flee <note>original: "fugiant"</note>, and the Work be annihilated. Also beware of the Scent, lest by chance it should exhale through any Crack, because the Strength of the Stone would suffer a notable Injury therefrom. Wherefore the Philosopher commands the Vessel to be kept cautiously with its Binding <note>The airtight seal or "Lute of Hermes"</note>, and be warned: do not cease from the Work, nor move the Vessel, nor open it, nor interrupt the Boiling <note>original: "Decoctionem"</note> at any Time, but continue by boiling until you see the Moisture fail, which will happen within thirty Days; then rejoice, and be certain that you have entered the right Path. Keep a watch on the Work, because within perhaps two Weeks from that Time you will see the whole Earth dry and remarkably black. Then the **Death of the Compound** is at hand; the Winds have ceased, and all things have given themselves to Rest. This is that great **Eclipse of the Sun and Moon** <note>The Sun represents the gold/sulfur, and the Moon represents the silver/mercury; their "eclipse" is the total blackness of the Nigredo stage</note> at once, in which no Light will shine over the Earth, and the Sea will disappear. Then our **Chaos** is prepared, from which, by the command of GOD, all the Wonders of the World will emerge in their Order.

### Regarding Chapter XX.

In this Chapter is explained the **Long Way** with its Colors, where **Gold** is joined with **Mercury**, or the **Corascene Dog** <note>original: "Canis Corascenus" - a symbolic name for a specific mineral or metallic reactant</note> with the **Armenian Bitch** <note>original: "Canicula Armenia" - likely referring to a specific grade of mercury or a reacting agent</note>, and they are placed in a Sealed Vessel for **Digestion**.

<summary>This page describes the transition of the alchemical matter into the Nigredo or "Blackness" stage, characterized by the appearance of colorful "Winds" (vapors) and the eventual drying of the substance into a black "Chaos." It emphasizes the necessity of a sealed vessel and constant heat to ensure the "Royal Offspring" is not lost to evaporation.</summary>

## CHAPTER XXI.
### *Concerning the Burning of the Flowers and the Caution regarding it.*

**A** significant error, and yet one easily committed, is the **Burning of the Flowers** <note>In alchemy, "flowers" refers to the refined, volatile substances or sublimates that rise to the top of the vessel</note>, before the tender Natures are properly extracted from their Depth. This error is especially to be guarded against after the third week. For in the beginning, there is such an abundance of **Moisture** <note>original: "Humoris"</note> that if you rule the Work with a stronger Fire than is appropriate, the fragile Vessel will not bear the abundance of Vapors <note>literally "Winds" (Ventorum)</note>, but will immediately shatter—unless by chance your Vessel is excessively large. And then indeed the Moisture will be scattered to such an extent that it will no longer return into its Body, at least not enough for its own refreshment. But when the **Earth** begins to retain a part of its **Water**, then indeed, as the Vapors fail, the Fire can be intensified beyond measure without any harm to the Vessel; however, the Work will thereby be spoiled, and it will give off the color of the **Wild Poppy** <note>original: "Papaveris Sylvestris"</note>, and the entire Compound will finally become a dry Powder, reddened to no purpose. You shall judge by this sign that the fire was stronger than just—namely, a heat that was an enemy to the true Union. For you should know that our Work requires a true **Change of Natures** <note>original: "Naturarum Mutationem"</note>, which cannot happen unless the final Union of both Natures occurs; but they cannot be united except in the Form of Water. For there is no union of Bodies; there is at most a crushing together <note>original: "Contusio"</note>, nor can there be a union of Body with Spirit by their **smallest parts** <note>original: "per Minima"—a technical term for union at the atomic or most fundamental level</note>; but Spirits can indeed be well united among themselves.

<meta>catchword: Change</meta>

---

<page-num>77</page-num>
<header>To the Closed Palace of the King.</header>

Change, which cannot happen unless the final Union of both Natures occurs; but they cannot be united except in the Form of Water. Therefore a **Homogeneous Metallic Water** is required, for which the way is prepared by a previous **Calcination** <term>Calcination: The process of heating a substance to high temperatures to reduce it to a powder or "ash," used here to prep the metal for union</term>. This **Drying** <note>original: "Exsiccatio"</note>, therefore, is not truly a drying, but a reduction of the Water with the Earth through the "Sieve of Nature" into atoms finer than the requirement of the Water brings, so that the Earth may receive the transmutative **Ferment** of the Water. But by a heat more violent than is proper, this Spiritual nature—struck as if by the Hammer of Death—is changed from Active to Passive, from Spiritual to Corporal, namely into a useless **Red Precipitate**; because in its proper heat, the color becomes that of **Raven-Blackness** <note>original: "Corvinæ Nigredinis"</note>, which, although dark, is the color most to be desired. **Redness**, however, is conspicuous at the beginning of the true Work, and it is remarkable; yet this occurs when the proper abundance of moisture is present, and it shows that **Heaven** has lain with **Earth**, and has conceived the **Fire of Nature**, and therefore the whole hollow of the glass will be tinged with a **Golden Color**; but this color will not last; but it will briefly give birth to **Green**, then **Black** within a time...

<meta>catchword: ...time</meta>

<summary>This page warns the alchemist against using excessive heat too early (the "Burning of the Flowers"), which results in a useless red powder rather than the desired "Raven-Blackness." It explains that the substances must be united in a liquid, "spiritual" state before they can undergo a true transformation.</summary>
<keywords>Burning of the Flowers, Calcination, Raven-Blackness, Red Precipitate, Metallic Water, alchemy, fire regulation, chemical union</keywords>

--- Page 72 ---
<meta>This page continues the specific instructions for managing the "Fire" during the alchemical process, warning the practitioner to maintain a steady heat to reach the stage of "blackness" or putrefaction without rushing to a premature "redness."</meta>

<page-num>78</page-num>
<header>The Open Entrance</header>
<meta>catchword: prevent-</meta>

...wait a little while, and if you are patient, you will see your **Desire**. At least **make haste slowly** <note>original: "festina lente"</note>, and yet maintain a sufficiently strong **Fire**, and between **Scylla and Charybdis** <note>A metaphor for the middle path: the practitioner must balance between too much and too little heat</note>, like a skilled Pilot, direct your Ship, if you wish to gain the **Riches of Both Indies** <note>Refers to the ultimate wealth and perfection of the Philosopher’s Stone</note>. From time to time you will see, as it were, small **Islands**, emitting multi-colored **Ears of grain** and **Umbrels** <note>umbrella-shaped clusters; original: "Umbellas"</note> in the Waves and at the Sides, which will shortly be dissolved, and others will arise. For the **Earth**, eager to germinate, is always producing something; sometimes you will imagine you see **Birds** or **Beasts** and **Reptiles** in the Glass, and **Colors** pleasant to the sight and of slight duration. The whole secret is that you constantly maintain the required **Fire**, and all these things—manifesting in a **most black Color**, a discontinuous Powder—will be finished before **Fifty Days** have passed. If not, you shall blame either your **Mercury**, or your **Governance**, or the **Disposition of your Matter**, unless by chance you have moved or shaken the Glass, which will easily prolong the Work, or even finally destroy it.

## Regarding Chapter XXI.

These things must be noted concerning the **Governance of Fire**, namely that the Fire should not be increased too much, because the Matter placed for **Digestion** would be corrupted by violent Heat and would pass into the form of a **Red Powder**. This would be to cause **Coagulation** before **Solution**, which

---

<page-num>79</page-num>
<header>To the King's Closed Palace.</header>
<meta>catchword: you,</meta>

must be prevented; for the **Body of Gold** must first be dissolved in **Mercury** through Digestion, and once it is dissolved, the Mercury is then coagulated.

# CHAPTER XXII.
## The Governance of Saturn: What it is and why it is so called.

As many of the **Magi** as have written about this **Sophyic Labor** <note>The "Wise" or "Philosophical" work of alchemy</note> have spoken of the **Work and Governance of Saturn**, whom some—understanding them wrongly—have wandered into various Errors and deceived themselves by their own Opinion. Some, led astray by this, have worked in literal **Lead** <note>original: "Plumbo"</note>, with the greatest Hope but the smallest Fruit. But know that **Our Lead** is more worthy than any Gold. It is the **Slime** <note>original: "Limus"</note> in which the **Soul of Gold** is joined with **Mercury**, so that they may afterwards produce **Adam** and his wife **Eve** <note>Alchemical symbols for the perfected male (Sulfur) and female (Mercury) principles</note>. Wherefore, since the **Highest** has humbled itself here to become the **Lowest**, one must await the **Redemption** of all its Brothers in its own **Blood**. Therefore, the **Tomb** in which our **King** is buried is called **Saturn** in our Work, and it is the **Key** to the Treasures of the Art. Happy is the man who can greet this **Slow-walking Planet** <note>original: "Tardambulonem"; a reference to the planet Saturn, which moves the slowest across the sky, symbolizing the slow, dark stage of the alchemical process</note>. Ask God, Brother, that He may deem you worthy of this Blessing, because it is not for those who run...

<summary>The author warns against excessive heat that causes premature coagulation and describes the transition into the "Regimen of Saturn," a stage of blackness and symbolic death (putrefaction) necessary for the Gold's "redemption."</summary>
<keywords>Governance of Fire, Saturn, Nigredo, Blackness, Alchemy, Mercury, Gold, Adam and Eve, Putrefaction, Scylla and Charybdis</keywords>

...you; not only from your own will, but from the **Father of Lights** <note>A biblical reference to God as the source of all enlightenment and success</note> alone does this Blessing depend.

### Regarding Chapter XXII.

In this Chapter, he encompasses the **Shorter Way** <note>original: "Viam Breviorem"; a method of achieving the Stone faster than the standard "Long Way"</note>, namely if **Saturn** <note>A term often used for lead, but here associated with Antimony</note> is taken—which comes from **Antimony**—and from it the **Philosophical Mineral** is made, then **Gold** will be resolved with easy effort and in less Time than is otherwise customary in the **Long Way**.

## CHAPTER XXIII.

### Concerning the different Governments of this Work.

Trust for certain, **Studious Novice** <note>original: "Tyro"</note>, that nothing in the whole **Work of the Stone** has been hidden except the **Government** <note>original: "Regimen"; the precise management of heat and time</note>, concerning which that saying of the Philosopher is true: *Whoever shall know it scientifically, the Princes and Great Men of the Earth will honor him.* And I swear to you in good faith, that if this alone were proposed openly, Fools themselves would laugh at the **Art**. For once it is known, the whole thing is nothing other than **Women's Work** and **Children's Play** <note>A common alchemical phrase meaning that once the initial secret is understood, the physical work is as simple as boiling water or playing games</note>, which is to decoct <note>To boil or extract through heat</note>. Therefore, the **Wise** <note>original: "Sophi"</note> have hidden this Secret with the greatest skill; and you may firmly believe that we have fundamentally done the same. However, since I have proposed **Candor** <note>Honesty/Openness</note> in this little treatise and have promised it, it is incumbent that something
<meta>catchword: be done-</meta>

<page-num>81</page-num>
<header>To the Closed Palace of the King.</header>

be done, lest I fail the hope and labors of ingenious readers. Therefore, know that our **Government** in the whole Work is **One Linear** process—that is, to **Decoct** and **Digest**—and yet this one Government encompasses many others within itself, which the Envious have hidden under a diversity of names and described as if they were various operations. We, for the sake of our promised **Candor**, will make a far more clear manifestation, which you will confess to be an unusual honesty on our part in this matter.

## CHAPTER XXIV.

### Concerning the first Government of the Work, which is that of Mercury.

And first indeed we will speak of the **Government of Mercury**, which is a Secret never expressed by any of the Wise. They, for instance, began from the **Second Work** or the **Government of Saturn**, and revealed no Light to the **Novice** before the **Capital Sign of Blackness** <note>The "Nigredo" stage, the first official sign of success in the Great Work</note>. In this, that Good Man **Count Bernard of Treves** <note>A famous 15th-century alchemist</note> was silent, who in his Parable teaches that when the **King** comes to the **Fountain**, having left behind all Strangers, he enters the **Bath** alone, dressed in a **Golden Garment**, which he strips off and hands to **Saturn**, from whom [he receives] a black silk...
<sig>F</sig>
<meta>catchword: garment</meta>

<summary>The author explains that the secret of alchemy lies not in the materials, but in the "government" (the management of heat), and begins to describe the first stage involving Mercury and the "Shorter Way" using Antimony.</summary>

...receives. But [the author] does not teach in what space of time that **Golden Garment** is cast off, and therefore he remains silent regarding one entire **Government of Days** <note>original: "Regimen Dierum"; the specific schedule of heating</note>—perhaps **Forty**, or even sometimes **Fifty** days—during which time the wretched **Novices** <note>original: "Tyrones"</note>, without a guide, apply themselves to uncertain experiments. From the arrival of the **Blackness** <note>original: "Nigredo"; the first major stage of the Great Work, signifying the "death" or decomposition of the matter</note> until the end of the **Work**, new signs appearing daily sufficiently cheer the **Artist**; but I confess it is tedious to wander here for fifty days without a guide, a sign, or a path. Therefore, I say that the entire interval of time from the **First Ignition** until the **Blackness** is the **Government of Mercury**; of **Sophic Mercury** <note>original: "Mercurii Sophici"; Philosophical Mercury, the secret solvent of the alchemists</note>, I say, which alone operates through that whole time, while its **Companion** <note>Gold</note> remains dead for a suitable space of time—and no one before me has revealed this.

Therefore, once the **Materials** are joined—which are **Gold** <note>original: "Sol"</note> and **Our Mercury**—do not believe, like the common crowd of **Alchemists**, that the **Setting of the Sun** <note>A metaphor for the dissolution of Gold</note> will happen quickly. Truly, no; we have waited much and long before **Patience** was established between the **Water** and the **Fire** <note>The reconciliation of the volatile solvent and the fixed metal</note>. The **Envious** <note>A term for earlier alchemical authors who hid their secrets out of "envy" for the uninitiated</note> included this under a brief name when they called their matter in the **First Work** **Rebis**, that is, a **Thing** made from a **Double Thing** <note>original: "Res ex Re Bina"</note>, according to the **Poet**:

<meta>catchword: Res</meta>

---

<page-num>83</page-num>
<header>To the Closed Palace of the King</header>
<page-type>text</page-type>

> The thing is **Rebis**, two joined, yet still one,
> Dissolved so they are first either **Gold** <note>original: "Sol"</note> or the **Seeds of the Moon** <note>original: "Spermata Lunæ"; the silver-like lunar essence or the refined mercurial seed</note>.

Therefore, know for certain that although **Our Mercury** devours the **Gold**, it does not do so in the way that the **Philosophasters** <note>Pretenders or foolish dabblers in alchemy</note> think. For even if you join **Gold** with **Our Mercury**, you will recover the same safely and in possession of its former virtue after a year of waiting, unless you have **Decocted** <note>Boiled or refined using heat</note> it in a suitable **Degree of Fire**. Whoever asserts the contrary is no **Philosopher**. Those who are on the **Path of Error** think that it is a business of such light effort to dissolve **Bodies**, for they imagine that **Gold** immersed in **Sophic Mercury** is to be devoured in the blink of an eye. They misunderstand that passage of **Count Bernard of Trevis** <note>A famous 15th-century alchemist</note> concerning his **Golden Book** being irrecoverably immersed in the **Fountain**.

But how difficult a work it is to dissolve **Bodies**, those who have sweated over **Dissolution** can testify. I myself, therefore, who have been taught this quite often by the testimony of my own eyes, testify that it is a very ingenious thing to govern the **Fire** after the **Matter** is prepared—a fire which might duly dissolve the **Bodies** without the **Combustion of the Tinctures** <note>The burning or destruction of the color-giving essences of the work</note>. Pay attention, therefore, to my doctrine: take the **Body** which I have shown, and place it in the **Water of Our Sea** <note>The mercurial solvent</note>, and decoct it continually with the proper fire, so that the **Dew** and **Clouds** <note>The vapors produced during heating</note> may ascend and fall back...

<meta>catchword: Gut-</meta>

<summary>Philalethes discusses the overlooked "Government of Mercury," a 40-50 day period of heating required to dissolve Gold into the "Blackness" (Nigredo). He warns that this dissolution is a difficult, skillful process of fire management, debunking the idea that Gold dissolves instantly in the philosopher's solvent.</summary>

<page-num>84</page-num>
<header>The Open Entrance</header>

Drops fall day and night without interruption. You should know that in this **Circulation** <note>original: "Circulatione"; the continuous process of evaporation and condensation within a sealed vessel</note>, the **Mercury** <note>The volatile alchemical solvent</note> rises in its original nature and leaves the **Body** <note>The solid material, usually gold</note> below in its original nature, until after a long time the Body begins to retain some of the **Water** <note>The liquid mercury solvent</note>, and thus each begins to share in the properties of the other. Because the whole of the Water does not rise through **Sublimation** <note>The process of vaporizing and condensing</note>, but a portion of it remains below with the Body at the bottom of the vessel, the Body is boiled and filtered with watchful diligence in the settling water. Through this process, the falling drops pierce the remaining mass, and the water—made more subtle by constant circulation—finally extracts the **Soul of the Sun** <note>original: "Solis Animam"; the inner essence or "seed" of gold</note> gently and smoothly. Thus, by means of the Soul, the **Spirit** is reconciled with the Body, and a union of both occurs in the **Black Color** <note>The stage of Nigredo, or decomposition</note>. This happens at most within **Fifty Days**, and this operation is called the **Government of Mercury**, because the Mercury circulates above while the Body of the Sun boils below. In this work, the Body is passive until the appearance of the colors, which begin to appear sparingly around the **Twentieth Day** with a good and continuous boiling. These colors then increase, multiply, and vary until they reach completion in the Blackest...

...**Blackness**, which the **Fiftieth Day** will grant you, if the **Fates** call you.

## CHAPTER XXV.

### Concerning the Second Regime of the Work, which belongs to Saturn.

When the **Regime of Mercury** <term>Regimen Mercurii: The initial period of boiling and circulation where the solvent acts upon the metallic body</term> is completed—the work of which is to strip the King of his Golden Garments, and to agitate and provoke the **Lion** <note>The "fixed" or solid part of the matter</note> with various conflicts until he reaches extreme exhaustion—the **Regime of Saturn** <term>Regimen Saturni: The stage of putrefaction and blackness, named after the heavy, dark planet Saturn</term> appears next. For **GOD** wills that the work begun be led to its proper end, and the law of this stage is that the exit of one is the entrance of another, the end of one is the origin of another; nor does the *Regime of Mercury* fade away any sooner than its successor, *Saturn*, enters for himself, having obtained the empire by right of succession. When the **Lion dies**, the **Raven** <note>A traditional symbol for the black stage of alchemy, representing death and decay</note> is born. This regime is quite uniform <note>original: "Lineare"; suggesting a steady, unchanging state</note> with respect to the heat, because there is only one color, and that the darkest black; yet no fumes, nor winds, nor any symbol of life are seen, but the composite material is observed, sometimes dried out, and sometimes boiling like liquid pitch. O sad spectacle and image of **Eternal Death**, yet sweet news to the Artificer! For the **Blackness** <note>original: "Nigredo"</note> seen is not just any blackness, but one shining with the most intense darkness. And when, like swelling dough, [it begins to rise...]

At least be careful with the Fire, which you are bound to rule here with sound judgment; and I swear to you in good faith, that if by pushing the Fire in this regimen you cause anything to sublimate <note>To transition directly from solid to vapor; in alchemy, this often meant the matter escaping the process too early</note>, you will lose the entire Work irrecoverably. Be content, therefore, to be detained with the good Trevisan <note>original: "Trevisano" – referring to Bernard Trevisan, a 15th-century alchemist who famously described the "prison" of the chemical vessel</note> in Prison for forty days and nights, and permit the tender Matter to remain in the Bottom, which is the Nest of Conception. Trust for certain that after the period appointed by the Almighty for this operation is completed, the Spirit will rise glorious, and will glorify its Body; it will ascend, I say, and circulate gently and without violence, and from the Center it will ascend to the Heavens, and again from the Heavens it will descend to the Center, and it will seize the Power of things Superior and Inferior <note>This is a direct reference to the Emerald Tablet of Hermes Trismegistus, describing the unification of spiritual and physical forces</note>.

## CHAPTER XXVI.
### Concerning the Regimen of Jupiter.

Black Saturn <note>Saturn is the alchemical symbol for lead and the color black, representing the "nigredo" or putrefaction stage</note> is succeeded by Jupiter, who is of a different Color. For after the proper putrefaction and the Conception has taken place in the

<meta>catchword: Va-</meta>

---

<page-num>89</page-num>
<header>To the Closed Palace of the King</header>

Bottom of the Vessel <note>original: "Vasis Fundo"</note>, by God's command, you will again see changing colors and a circulating Sublimation. This Regimen is not lasting, nor does it endure beyond Three Weeks. During this time, all imaginable colors will appear, for which no certain explanation can be given. During these days, the rains <note>Condensation within the flask</note> will multiply daily, and finally, after all these most beautiful sights, a Whiteness <note>original: "Albedo"</note> shows itself at the sides of the Vessel like streaks or hairs. Then rejoice, because you have successfully completed the Regimen of Jupiter. In this Regimen, let the greatest caution be observed, lest the Young Crows <note>original: "Corvorum Pulli" – a common alchemical metaphor for the black, soot-like matter of the nigredo stage</note>, after they have left their nest, return to the same. Also, do not draw off the Water so immoderately that the sinking Earth lacks it, and is left dry and useless at the bottom. Thirdly, do not irrigate your Earth so intemperately that you utterly drown it. All these errors will be remedied by the good management of external heat.

The **Regimen of Jupiter** <note>In alchemy, the "Regimen of Jupiter" refers to a stage of the work where the matter undergoes a sequence of many changing colors, often called the "Peacock’s Tail."</note> was dedicated to the washing of *Latona* <note>original: "Latoni"; in alchemy, Latona is a symbolic name for the impure matter or "mother" of the sun and moon that must be purified</note>. The washing Spirit is very White in its own Nature, but the Body to be washed is Black, the Blackest black. In its Transition to Whiteness, all the Intermediate Colors appeared; when these are finished, the whole becomes White, yet it does not shine with perfect Whiteness on the first Day, but rather it will rise up gradually from white to the Whitest white. And you should know that in this Regimen, the whole appears to the sight like liquid **Quicksilver** <note>original: "Argenti Vivi" or Mercury</note>, and this is called the *Sealing of the Mother in the Belly of her Infant* <note>A metaphorical description of the chemical union and containment of the "mercurial" spirit within the "sulfurous" body</note> whom she has brought forth; and in this Regimen there will be various beautiful, momentary colors, quickly disappearing, yet closer to Whiteness than to Blackness—just as the Colors in the Regimen of Jupiter participated more in Blackness than in Whiteness. And know that within three Weeks the **Regimen of the Moon** <note>original: "Regimen Lunæ"; the stage where the matter reaches a stable, brilliant white state representing silver</note> will be completed. 

But before it is finished, the Compound will take on a thousand Forms. For as the "Rivers" <note>The liquid components within the flask</note> swell before all-encompassing Coagulation, it will melt and coagulate a Hundred times in a Day; sometimes it will appear like **Fish Eyes**, and sometimes it will mimic the Shape of a pure, most polished **Silver Tree** with little Branches and Leaves. In a word, at this Time, every Hour the things you see will overwhelm you with Stupor and Admiration. And finally, the gra-

Let us give immortal Thanks to our GOD, who has brought the Work to this point. For it is a true **Tincture** <note>An alchemical substance capable of transforming or "dyeing" other metals</note> perfected to the White, though only of the First Order, and therefore of small Virtue in respect to the wonderful Virtue it will acquire through repeated Preparation.

# CHAPTER XXVIII.
## Regarding the Regimen of Venus.

Above all things, this is a wonder: that **Our Stone**, now perfected in every way and capable of communicating a perfect Tincture, humbles itself again of its own accord, and will meditate upon a new Volatility without any Laying on of Hands <note>That is, without the alchemist needing to manually intervene or add new substances</note>. If, however, you take it out of its Vessel, and this same Stone is enclosed again in another Vessel after it has cooled, it will be attempted in vain to lead it any further. Of this, neither we nor any of the Ancient Philosophers are able to give a demonstrative Reason, except that it is done by the Will of GOD. At least here, beware of your Fire, because this is the Law of the **Perfected Stone**: that it be **Fusible** <note>Capable of being melted</note>. Therefore, if you give it a Fire greater than what is Just, the Matter will be **vitrified** <note>Turned into a useless glass-like substance</note>, and having melted against the si-

## CHAPTER XXIX.
### *Concerning the Regimen of Mars.*

This regimen demonstrates a certain Yellowness, diluted as if with a yellowish-brown darkness, and most gloriously displays the transitory colors of the **Rainbow and the Peacock** <note>original: "Iridis ac Pavonis"; the "Peacock's Tail" is a famous alchemical stage of iridescent, shifting colors</note>. Here the state of the Compound is drier, in which the Matter seems to imitate various masks of forms. A Hyacinth color <note>a blue-violet or reddish-blue</note> with a very light Orange will frequently appear in these days. Here the **Sealed Mother** <note>A recurring metaphor for the "mercurial" or feminine principle being contained and transformed</note> rises in the **Belly of her Infant** and is purified, so that because of such great Purity in which the Compound is placed, putrefaction is exiled from here. # CHAPTER XXX.
## *Regarding the Regimen of the Sun.*

Now you are approaching the end of your work, and you have almost completed your business; everything now looks like **Refined Gold** <note>original: "Auri Obryzi"; gold of the highest purity</note>, and the **Virgin's Milk** <note>original: "Lac Virginis"; the mercurial liquid used to "nourish" the matter</note>—with which you soak this matter—turns deeply yellow. Give immortal thanks now to GOD, the Giver of all good things, who has brought the work this far. Pray to Him as a suppliant that He may guide your counsel from here on, lest by striving to hurry the work when it is almost finished, you lose everything completely. Consider now that you have waited nearly seven months, and it would not be wise to annihilate everything in a single hour. Therefore be very cautious, and all the more so the closer you are to Perfection. Proceeding

but cautiously, these notable things will occur to you: First, you will observe a certain **Citrine Sweat** on the Body, and finally Citrine Vapors as the body subsides, tinged with Violet and sometimes a dark Purple. Finally, around the twenty-sixth day of this Regimen, it will begin to dry out, and then it will liquefy and congeal, and liquefy again a hundred times a day until it begins to turn into grains. It will appear as if it is entirely broken into disconnected grains, and then it will unite again, putting on infinite masks of forms from day to day; this will last for about two weeks. At last, by God's command, a Light will shine upon your matter such as you can hardly imagine. Then expect the End quickly, which you will see after three days, because the matter will granulate like **Atoms of the Sun** <note>original: "Atomorum Solis"; likely referring to dust motes dancing in a sunbeam</note>. The color will be so intensely Red that, due to its pre-eminent redness, it will look black, like the healthiest **clotted blood** <note>original: "Sanguinis sanissimi coagulati"</note>; though you might not believe such a thing could be produced by Art for this **Elixir** <note>The perfected Philosopher's Stone</note>. # CHAPTER XXXI.
## *The Fermentation of the Stone.*

Remember that you have now obtained the **Red Incombustible Sulfur** <note>term: "Sulphur Incombustibile Rubeum"; the stable, fiery core of the Philosopher's Stone that can no longer be destroyed by heat</term>, which could not be advanced any further by itself through any fire whatsoever. Be extremely cautious—as I had forgotten to mention in the preceding chapter—lest in the **Regimen of the Citrine Sun** <note>the yellowing stage of the process</note>, before the Arrival of the **Supernatural Son** <note>the final, perfected state of the matter</note>, who is clothed in a true **Tyrian Color** <note>a deep, royal purple-red</note>; lest, I say,

<meta>catchword: Your</meta>

---

<page-num>97</page-num>
<header>Toward the Closed Palace of the King.</header>

**Your Matter** <note>original: "Tuam Materiam"</note> be turned into glass by undue ignition <note>vitrification; if the heat is too high or the proportions wrong, the matter becomes a useless, glassy slag</note>, because it would thereafter be insoluble, and consequently it would not congeal into those most beautiful, deep red atoms. Therefore, be cautious that you do not deprive yourself of such a great Treasure. And yet, do not believe that you have found the end of your labors here, but rather proceed further, so that from this **Sulfur** <note>the dry, active principle</note> you may have the **Elixir** through a repeated **Circulation of the Wheel** <note>the process of redistilling or repeating the cycle of heating and cooling</note>. 

Therefore, take three parts of the most purified **Sun** <note>term: "Solis"; gold</term>, and one part of this **Fiery Sulfur** (you could take four parts of the Sun and a fifth part of Sulfur, but the aforementioned proportion is better); melt the Gold in a clean crucible, and cast into it your Sulfur while it is in flux—but do so cautiously, lest it be lost to the smoke of the coals. Make them flow together, then pour them into a receiving vessel, and you will have a mass that can be ground into powder, possessing a most beautiful, deep red color, yet barely transparent. Take one part of this mass, finely ground, and two parts of your **Philosophical Mercury** <note>term: "Mercurii tui Sophici"; the refined liquid agent of the work, not common mercury</note>; mix them perfectly and seal them in glass. Regulate the heat as before, and within two months you will see all the previously mentioned Regimens pass by in their proper order. This is the **True Fermentation** <note>the stage where the Stone is "multiplied" in power and quantity</note>, which you may repeat again if you wish.

# CHAPTER XXXII.
## The Imbibition of the Stone.

I know that many authors take **Fermentation** in this Work to mean an internal, invisible Agent, which they call the **Ferment**; by its virtue, the fleeing and subtle Spirits are spontaneously thickened without the "imposition of hands" <note>This implies the process happens naturally within the sealed vessel without manual interference by the alchemist.</note>. They call our aforementioned way of Fermentation a "**Feeding with Bread and Milk**"; so says **Ripley** <note>Sir George Ripley (c. 1415–1490), an influential English alchemist. The "Bread and Milk" metaphor refers to feeding the solid "Stone" with liquid "mercurial water."</note>. I, however, am not accustomed to citing others, nor to swearing by their words in a matter as well known to me as it is to them; I have observed my own liberty. There is, therefore, another operation,

<meta>catchword: tion,</meta>

---

<page-num>99</page-num>
<header>To the Secret Palace of the King.</header>
<sig>G 2</sig>

tion, by which the **Stone** is increased in weight more than in virtue <note>In alchemy, "virtue" refers to the potency or "tinging" power, while "weight" refers to the physical quantity of the material.</note>. That is: take your **Perfect Sulfur**, whether White or Red, and add to three parts of the **Sulfur** a fourth part of **Water** <note>Sulfur and Water here refer to the solid and liquid components of the alchemical matter, not common brimstone or tap water.</note>. After a little bit of blackness, following six or seven days of boiling <note>original: "Decoctione"</note>, your recently added Water will be thickened like your Sulfur. Then add another fourth part—not in respect to the whole compound (which has already coagulated one-fourth part), but in respect to the Sulfur as it was before the first **Imbibition** <note>Imbibition: the act of the dry Stone "drinking" the liquid Mercurial water.</note>. When this is dried out, add another portion in respect to the three parts of Sulfur weighed before the first Imbibition. In this proportion, you should soak and congeal three times. Finally, for the seventh Imbibition, you should add five parts of Water, namely in respect to the Sulfur first taken. Once this is added, seal your **Vessel**, and with a heat similar to the previous one, ensure that the whole compound passes through all the aforementioned **Regimes** <note>The "Regimes" are the stages of color changes (Black, White, etc.) that the matter must repeat.</note>. This will be done in one month at most; then you have the true **Stone of the Third Order**, of which one part falls upon ten thousand, and will perfectly tinge <note>Tinge: the ability of the Stone to transform base metals into gold or silver. A 1:10,000 ratio indicates a very high purity.</note>.

## Regarding Chapter XXXII.

On **Imbibition**, one should consult **Flamel**, pages 69, 70, 73, 71, 105. **Dienheim**, pages 128, 118, 119. **Resschen’s** *Osiandrian Experiments* <note>original: "Osiandrische Experiment"</note>, page 164. *The Chemical Theater*, Volume IV, page 548, etc.

<header>The Open Entrance</header>

## CHAPTER XXXIII.
### The Multiplication of the Stone.

To this, no other labor is required except that the **perfect Stone** be taken, and one part of it be joined with three, or at most four, parts of the **Mercury of the first Work** <note>This refers to the specialized alchemical solvent or "water" prepared at the beginning of the process, rather than common quicksilver.</note>, and governed by the proper **Fire** for seven days, in a vessel very tightly closed. All the **Regimens** <note>The specific stages of heat and color changes (the Black, White, and Red stages).</note> will pass by with the greatest joy, and you will have the whole enriched with a thousand-fold virtue beyond what the Stone possessed before its **Multiplication**. And if you attempt this again, you will run through all the Regimens in three days, and the **Medicine** will be exalted by yet another thousand-fold in its power of **Tinging** <note>The "tinging power" is the ability of the Stone to "dye" or transform the internal nature of a base metal into gold.</note>. And if you still desire to repeat it, you will lead the work through all the Regimens and colors within a single **Natural Day** <note>A 24-hour period.</note>; and the same will be done in a **Single Hour** if you attempt it yet again. Indeed, you will never be able to find the ultimate limit of your Stone’s virtue; it will be so great that it surpasses the capacity of the human mind, provided you persevere in this work. Be mindful now to give immortal thanks to **GOD**, because you now hold the entire **Treasury of Nature** in your possession.

<column-break/>

<page-num>101</page-num>
<header>To the Closed Palace of the King.</header>

### Regarding Chapter XXXIII.

Regarding **Multiplication**, besides our own author and our *Mineralogy*, one should read: *The Hermetic Ark of Philosophy* <note>original: "Arca Hermet. Philosoph."</note>, pages 10, 26, 47, 56, 60, 15. **Reschen’s** *Osiandrian Experiment* <note>original: "Osiandrische Experiment"</note>, pages 66, 67. **Figulus’s** *Olympic Rose Garden* <note>original: "Rosarium Olymp."</note>, pages 112, 113, 114, 115. *The Chemical Theater* <note>original: "Theatri Chymici"</note>, Volume IV, page 408; Volume II, page 116; Volume VI, page 150 following, 429. **Basil Valentine**, page 67. **Walch’s** *Little Peasant* <note>original: "Kleiner Bauer"</note>, page 289. **Dienheim**, pages 132, 133, 210. **Isaac Hollandus** on the *Philosopher's Stone* <note>original: "de L. Ph." (de Lapide Philosophorum)</note>, page 52. By the same author, *The Philosophers’ Hand* <note>original: "Hand der Philosophen"</note>, pages 136, 137. **Flamel**, pages 121, 122. *Geber Reborn* <note>original: "Geber Redivivus"</note>, page 41 following, etc.

## CHAPTER XXXIV.
### Regarding the Method of Projection.

Take your **Perfect Stone**, as has been said, either the **White** or the **Red**, and according to the quality of the Medicine, take four parts of either **Luminary** <note>The "Luminaries" are Gold (the Sun) and Silver (the Moon). Use gold for the Red Stone and silver for the White Stone.</note>; melt them in a clean **Crucible**, then put in your Stone according to the type of the fused Luminary (White or Red). Once mixed, pour it into a **Cone** <note>A conical casting mold used to separate the metal from the slag.</note>, and it will be a mass that can be ground into powder. Take one part of this mixture and ten parts of well-washed **Mercury**; heat the Mercury until it begins to crackle <note>original: "strepere"</note>, then throw in your mixture, which will penetrate it in the **blink of an eye**. Melt it with an increased fire, and the whole will be the **Medicine of the inner order**. Of this, then take one part...

<summary>This page explains the final practical steps of alchemy: Multiplication, which exponentially increases the Stone's potency through repeated cycles, and Projection, the method of mixing the Stone with gold or silver to create the final transformative powder.</summary>
<keywords>Multiplication, Projection, Tinging Power, Medicine of the inner order, Crucible, Luminaries, Mercury of the first work, Philalethes, Alchemy</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for the final stages of the Great Work. Following the previous page's discussion on Multiplication, we now conclude the instructions for Projection (the transformation of base metals) and begin a summary of the spiritual and practical benefits of the Stone.</meta>

<page-num>102</page-num>
<header>The Open Entrance</header>

...one [part of the Stone], and project it upon any metal, molten and purified, exactly as much as your Stone is able to **tinge** <term>Tinge: The alchemical process of "dyeing" or transforming a base metal into a precious one by introducing the Stone.</term>, and you will have **Gold** or **Silver** so pure that Nature will not provide anything purer. However, it is better to project it gradually until the **Tincture** <term>Tincture: The active principle of the Philosopher's Stone that causes transmutation; it refers to the Stone's ability to permeate and change the nature of other substances.</term> ceases, for in this way it will be spread more widely; because when a tiny amount is projected onto so much, unless the **Projection** <term>Projection: The final step of the Great Work, where a small amount of the Stone is cast into molten base metal to transform it into gold or silver.</term> is made in **Mercury** <note>original: "in Mercurio"</note>, a notable loss of the **Medicine** <note>The Stone is often called the "Medicine" of metals, as it "heals" them of their imperfections.</note> occurs because of the dross which clings to unclean metals. Therefore, the better the metals are purified before Projection, the better the Work succeeds in the **Fire**.

### Regarding Chapter XXXIV

**Flamel** <note>Nicholas Flamel (c. 1330–1418), the famous French scrivener and legendary alchemist.</note> (p. 121, 107, 31), Cocheim (p. 68), *The Root of Chemistry* <note>original: "Radix Chymiæ"</note> (p. 152), Isaac Hollandus regarding the *Stone of the Philosophers* <note>original: "de L. Ph." (Lapis Philosophorum)</note> (p. 100, 140), the same author's *Hand of the Philosophers* (p. 10, 155, 118, 119, 120), Walchius in the place already cited (p. 289), Dienheim (p. 132), *The Chemical Theater* <note>The "Theatrum Chemicum," a massive multi-volume compendium of alchemical texts published in the 17th century.</note> Vol. VI (p. 551), Vol. II (p. 175), etc., have all given this chapter elegantly explained.

# CHAPTER XXXV.
## Concerning the Multiple Uses of this Art.

He who has once perfectly completed this Art according to the Blessing of **GOD**, I do not know what he could desire in this whole world, except that...
<meta>catchword: except that</meta>

<column-break/>

<page-num>103</page-num>
<header>To the Open Palace of the King.</header>

...except that, safe from all evil fraud and deceit, he might continually serve his **GOD**. It would be vain, indeed the vainest of all things, to pant for the common air of pomp <note>original: "Pompa vulgarem Auram anhelare"; to seek public glory or vanity.</note>, for such things are not in the hearts of those who are skilled in this Art; rather, they despise and look down upon them. He who is therefore blessed by **GOD** with this talent, for him such a **Field of Pleasure** lies open:

First, if he were to live a thousand years and feed a thousand thousand men daily, he would not lack for anything, because according to his wish he is able to **multiply** the Stone as much in weight as in virtue <note>The Stone's "virtue" refers to its potency or strength.</note>. So that he could—if he had this in his wishes—**tinge** all the comparable imperfect metals that are in the world into true **Gold** or **Silver**.

Second, he will be able to make **Precious Stones** and **Gems** by this Art, such as none can be compared in the nature of things without this Art.

Third and finally, he has the **Universal Medicine** <term>Universal Medicine: An alchemical elixir believed to cure all bodily ailments and prolong life indefinitely.</term> for all diseases, so that even a single true **Adept** <term>Adept: A master alchemist who has successfully discovered and prepared the Philosopher's Stone.</term> could cure all the sick in the entire world.

Therefore, let us give praise for these gifts of His to the **Eternal, Immortal, and only Almighty King** forever.

Whoever therefore enjoys this talent, in...

I advise that he use this knowledge for the honor of GOD and the Utility of his Neighbor, lest he be found ungrateful toward GOD the Creditor, who blessed him with such a great Talent <note>A reference to the Biblical Parable of the Talents (Matthew 25), suggesting that the knowledge of alchemy is a wealth entrusted by God that must be invested for good.</note>; and lest he be found guilty and condemned on the Last Day.

It will also not be a burden here to refer to our own *Mineralogy*, in which we have examined the most important points of this Chapter with as much clarity as possible, and reviewed the Virtues of Metals, which exert themselves most in the <term>Chemical Art</term> <note>At this time, "Chemistry" and "Alchemy" were often interchangeable terms for the study of the transformation of matter.</note>. To which, if the Eternal Divinity <note>original: "æternum Numen"</note> breathes upon it and grants Strength and Life, we will soon join our **Pandora** <note>Likely referring to a planned or existing manuscript by the commentator, titled after the mythological figure but representing a collection of "all gifts" or secrets.</note>, which, like a Commentary, will explain these very brief little observations on Philalethes a bit more extensively, and [will explain] these first lines more

# MASTER
## DOCTOR BECHER'S
### *The Unmasked Pantaleon* <note>original: "Pantaleon Delarvatus." This title refers to Franz Gassmann, an alchemist who wrote under the pseudonym "Pantaleon." Becher is "unmasking" him as a fraud.</note>.
### To the Chemistry-Loving Reader: May you attain the Truth you seek.

The wish that I desire for you, Kind Reader, is the Attainment of Truth; therefore, lest you receive coals instead of treasure <note>A Latin idiom, "pro thesauro carbones," meaning to be bitterly disappointed or cheated by a false promise.</note>, I have considered it the duty of my profession to uncover impostures for you, to pull off the masks, and to call a spade a spade <note>original: "scapham scapham vocare," literally "to call a skiff a skiff."</note>. How this is the duty of my profession will be made clear from a certain book soon to be published. I love the Kingdom of Christ, which is truth; everyone who is of the Truth hears that voice, and loves the light and flees the darkness. Thus, I have publicly illuminated the darkness of the Schools with my didactic Light; thus, I have intrepidly dissipated the clouds against P. and F. M. in the writing entitled: *Truth Defended against the Infamous Decree of the University of V. or Z.* <note>Likely referring to Vienna or Zurich; Becher was frequently embroiled in academic and religious disputes.</note>; thus, I have placed the confused state of Religion before the eyes of the world
<meta>catchword: eyes</meta>

---

<page-num>107</page-num>
<page-type>text</page-type>
<header>The Unmasked Pantaleon.</header>

under *Eight Fundamental Columns*; thus, I have explained the political state of the Roman Empire in a treatise on commerce; thus, I have explained many mathematical fallacies; thus, I have restored Physics, especially Chemistry, to Truth, and I have chastised the "Non-Entities" of Rolfinck <note>Werner Rolfinck (1599–1673), a German physician and chemist whom Becher criticized for his theoretical approach.</note> in my *Subterranean Physics*, in the *Century of Sophisticators*, in the *Glauberian Histories* <note>Referring to Johann Rudolf Glauber, a famous chemist whom Becher accused of being a "sophisticator" or deceiver.</note>, in the *Scilerian History*, and in other writings. Therefore, I must by no means leave this Crow [G—] <note>Refers to Gassmann/Pantaleon. Comparing a rival to a "crow" implies he is a thief of others' ideas or a loud, uneducated bird among philosophers.</note> wandering and flying about unpunished everywhere, attacking the best philosophers—especially our Philalethes—but I must stand by the truth, pluck the crow's feathers, and present him naked to the world. I hold solid documents in my hand for this purpose, lest this ambitious J. <note>Likely Johannes Gassmann.</note> impose any longer upon the world with his writings and upon Kings with his letters. I do this because I have been asked by many, and I have pledged again to our Langius—the first editor of the Philalethian writing in Hamburg—that I would do so. Since that time, here in England, I have found even more documents concerning our "Crow." I call God to witness that I do not write this against G— out of private passion, even though he attacks my *Solinum Salzthal* <note>Solinum Salzthal was a pseudonym used by Becher himself in his earlier alchemical writings.</note>, but because under the name of Pantaleon he has deceived so many men by using the truth of others,
<sig>O 2</sig>
<meta>catchword: truth,</meta>

<summary>Johann Joachim Becher introduces a polemic against the alchemist "Pantaleon" (Franz Gassmann), positioning himself as a universal defender of truth in science, religion, and politics. He vows to expose Pantaleon as a fraud who has plagiarized and insulted respected figures like Philalethes.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Becher's critique of alchemical frauds, specifically contrasting the genuine "Adept" Philalethes with the impostor "G--" (Gassmann). <page-num>108</page-num>
<header>Of Master Doctor Becher</header>

...clarity, whom he nevertheless despised, such as Philalethes <note>Eirenaeus Philalethes was a famous, then-anonymous alchemical author (now identified as George Starkey). Becher is defending his legitimacy.</note>; and indeed, I long investigated Philalethes himself to see if he too were an impostor, perhaps using the borrowed feathers of Suchten <note>Alexander von Suchten (c. 1520–1575), a respected alchemist who wrote on the "secrets of antimony." Becher wondered if Philalethes had simply stolen his ideas.</note>. But in England, I discovered for certain that Philalethes was a true Adept <term>Adept: A master alchemist who has successfully discovered the secret of the Philosopher's Stone.</term>, while G - - <note>Referring to Franz Gassmann, who wrote under the name "Pantaleon."</note> was not. It is therefore necessary to stand by the truth and resist falsehood. I know indeed that I would perform a most welcome service for the reader if I were to present as many twin proofs for the true existence of Philalethes as I might allege here regarding the fraud of G - -; however, it is necessary to publish the latter so that "Philochymists" <note>original: "Philochymici," meaning lovers of chemistry or alchemy.</note> may guard themselves against him and not be deceived. The former records, however, must be preserved, lest we cause harm or let good philosophers be ruined.

Meanwhile, it is to be hoped that all false writers and chemical compilers who pretend to be Adepts might be thus punished and exposed. They impose books upon their readers, piling up and confusing information; they serve up "twice-cooked cabbage" <note>original: "crambem bis coctam recoquant." A Latin idiom for recycling old, stale ideas and presenting them as new.</note>, and like drones, they suck the life out of the labors of others. They chase after unearned honors and hunt for foreign money. If such people were exposed, Chemistry would appear much purer and more beautiful. This will happen if other good men will collaborate with me, so that such "anonymous owls" <note>A metaphor for those who work in the shadows or hide their lack of true knowledge behind secrecy.</note> may be uncovered and forced to answer publicly before the judgment of truth and give an account of themselves.

<column-break/>

<page-num>109</page-num>
<header>The Unmasked Pantaleon.</header>

### State of the Case.

In the year 1676, a certain man named C - - G - -, by nation (if I am not mistaken) from M - -, a physician by profession and, as he proclaimed himself, a Graduate Doctor, often visited me. He was a young man, yet presumptuous in his speech. He sought my advice regarding a certain "Hypochondriac Melancholy" which he complained of having contracted in Hungary. Furthermore, he fell into chemical discourses concerning Sophic Mercury <term>Sophic Mercury: The "Philosophical Mercury," a theoretical substance believed to be the essential starting point for creating the Philosopher's Stone.</term>, probing that subject with objections in order to draw something out of me; for he knew that I was at that time very much occupied with the works of Suchten. 

Moreover, he told several stories about an anonymous metal extracted from iron, which looked like Gold, yet was not Gold—alluding to the "Physical Gold" of Philalethes or the "Sophistic Gold" of Suchten. He babbled various things concerning the homogeneity of Mercury, the casting down of its dregs like a black powder, and its digestion into Gold. In short, he left no part of Philosophy untouched, but everything was phrased with uncertainty, through the clever trick of merely asking questions or raising objections. Having enjoyed my familiarity in this way for several months, he finally vanished without saying goodbye. After about half a year, he returned...

<summary>Becher defends the legitimacy of the alchemist Philalethes while preparing to expose "G--" (Gassmann) as a fraud. He describes his early encounters with the young physician, who used leading questions about Sophic Mercury and iron-derived gold to probe Becher's own knowledge.</summary>
<keywords>Philalethes, Franz Gassmann, Alexander von Suchten, Adept, Sophic Mercury, alchemical fraud, Hypochondriac Melancholy, 1676</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the narrative of Johann Joachim Becher’s interactions with the man using the pseudonym "Pantaleon" (Franz Gassmann). The previous page established Becher's skepticism toward Gassmann, contrasted with his respect for the anonymous author Philalethes.</meta>

# Of Master Doctor Becher
<page-num>110</page-num>
<header>Of Master Doctor Becher</header>
<meta>catchword: au-</meta>

Upon returning, he secretly sent me his printed *Pantaleon* <note>Referring to one of Franz Gassmann’s works published under the pseudonym "Pantaleon," such as the *Bifolium Metallicum*.</note>, but with his name suppressed, through a certain interpreter known to me—an Alsatian by nationality named K--. This man, while presenting the booklet, indicated that Pantaleon himself was nearby and sent his greetings; he claimed to be a true Adept <term>Adept: A master alchemist who has successfully discovered the secret of the Philosopher's Stone.</term> and desired my friendship. I agreed that he should bring him, and finally our masked Pantaleon arrived in the form of G-- <note>Franz Gassmann.</note>. He immediately began to excuse himself for having departed without saying goodbye to his host, claiming that a sudden opportunity had been offered to him by an Adept, from whom P-- had learned the true art during that time. He had therefore changed his name, imploring me by all the gods above to keep his name secret if I wished to enjoy his company. He promised to hand everything over honestly, but claimed he lacked the necessary means. Consequently, he wished to offer his art to J-- and asked that if his petitioning booklet came to my hands for judgment, I would give it a white pebble <note>original: "album adderem calculum." An ancient metaphor for a favorable vote or approval, derived from the custom of using white stones for acquittal and black for conviction.</note>. I promised I would do so and I did; however, J-- did not accept the offer and returned the original memorial into my hands. Since the matter did not succeed as desired, he asked that I offer it to C. P. for a thousand ducats, but he also refused, especially because a certain Bohemian Doctor of Medicine named B. heard about this business of G--.

<column-break/>

<page-num>111</page-num>
<header>Pantaleon Unmasked.</header>
<meta>catchword: tur,</meta>

He ran to P., reporting that G-- was actually F-- <note>Likely another pseudonym or a known fraudster.</note>, and that whatever scientific knowledge he proposed to sell, he had learned from a certain religious, old, and poor man named V--. He had hidden with this old man for some time. Therefore, that old man was now offering his own services to C. P., and if he could only acquire enough to sustain his life, he would share everything. But P. believed neither of them. Consequently, G-- fled to P. C. E. L., a man deeply devoted to Chemistry <note>original: "Chymiam," referring to the inseparable field of chemistry and alchemy at the time.</note> and very generous. By offering his *Pantaleon* to this man, he received several ducats, with which he bought clothes, took a wife in marriage, and practiced medicine. He avoided my presence and never came into my sight again. In the meantime, I departed from V. <note>Likely Vienna, where Becher served at the Imperial Court.</note> and came to England. There, while examining certain letters written by a certain Matthias A-- from Ratisbon <note>Modern Regensburg.</note> to the King of England, I immediately recognized the character, style, and handwriting. I showed the same memorial that C-- had presented, and it was found to be exactly the same, of almost the same content. Indeed, the handwriting matched "as milk to milk" <note>original: "ut lac lacti conveniebat," a Latin idiom meaning a perfect or identical resemblance.</note>. Having seen, combined, and observed these things, I could no longer bear the man’s insolence—he who publicly attacks my Solinus Salzthalius <note>Becher wrote under the pseudonym "Solinus Salzthal" early in his career; thus, he is defending his own work here.</note> and Philalethes...

<summary>Becher recounts how Franz Gassmann (writing as Pantaleon) attempted to sell alchemical secrets to various patrons under false pretenses before Becher eventually identified his handwriting in fraudulent letters sent to the King of England.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Johann Joachim Becher’s polemic against Franz Gassmann (writing as "Pantaleon"). Becher is exposing the contradictions in Gassmann's letters to the English King compared to his published attacks on the alchemist Eirenaeus Philalethes.</meta>

# Of Master Doctor Becher
<page-num>112</page-num>
<header>Of Master Doctor Becher</header>
<meta>catchword: golden</meta>

...[principles] which he nevertheless uses in his own letters. I have decided that he must now be publicly corrected, so that he may no longer deceive Kings and Philosophers with his writings or letters, nor rave against the lovers of truth—namely, the Philalethans <note>Followers of Eirenaeus Philalethes, a famous anonymous alchemist of the era.</note>. And while these are the public and moral reasons for the "plucking of this Jackdaw" <note>An allusion to Aesop's fable of the jackdaw who dressed himself in the feathers of other birds to appear grander than he was.</note>, as far as the physical and philosophical reasons are concerned, I shall demonstrate an open lie and a massive contradiction in G— <note>Gassmann.</note>. Indeed, throughout the whole world, in his *Pantaleon* and his other pamphlets, he rails against Philalethes for using the Regulus of Mars <term>Regulus of Mars: A metallic form of antimony reduced using iron (Mars), a crucial material in many alchemical processes.</term>, even while he attempts to demonstrate there, with "wondrous skill," that no part of the Mars has entered the Regulus. He believes he has thus defeated Philalethes. Meanwhile, in his own letters to the King of Great Britain, this same G— frankly confesses that the animation of his Mercury proceeds from the Antimonial Regulus of Mars. For this "secret" he demands so many thousands of ducats, even though Philalethes, in the experiments of his Sophic Mercury <term>Sophic Mercury: The "Philosophical Mercury," a refined substance believed to be the starting point for the Philosopher's Stone.</term>, teaches this very same preparation for free, in words so clear that anyone might understand them. Furthermore, Alexander Seton <note>A famous 17th-century Scottish alchemist.</note> wrote an entire book on the theory and practice of this operation a century ago. Our little Jackdaw therefore proposes nothing but "warmed-over cabbage" <note>original: "Crambem recoctam." A Latin proverb for a stale, boring repetition of something already known.</note>, and for that he is surely worthy...

---

<page-num>113</page-num>
<header>Pantaleon Unmasked.</header>
<sig>H</sig>
<meta>catchword: these,</meta>

...of the highest dignity of the English Order of the Golden Garter, and indeed of knighthood itself! But truly, so that you might have something to take back with you, G—, as was written to you in response from England: behold this "plucked jackdaw" with which you wished to deceive J— and R—. You actually threaten the King of England in your letters, saying that unless he decides quickly, you will communicate the art to another Monarch—something you had already done long before, as is clear from your letters to J—. Thus, you not only deceive and attack Philosophical men, but you even threaten and lie to Monarchs and Kings. You act as if you were established as a second Paracelsus <note>The famous Swiss-German physician and alchemist; calling someone a "second Paracelsus" could be a high compliment or, as here, an accusation of arrogance.</note>, a Monarch over all Philosophers, to whom is granted dominion over both Kings and Sages. You miserable man! "If you had kept silent, you would have remained a philosopher" <note>original: "si tacuisses, Philosophus mansisses." A famous Boethian proverb suggesting that someone's foolishness is only revealed when they speak.</note>. See now how you wander like a fool among philosophers with a "leaden sword" <note>original: "plumbea machæra." A metaphor for a dull wit or a weak argument.</note>—to use your own Latin—prostituting yourself like a trickster. If God had truly granted this art to you, you ought to have remained silent and enjoyed it in piety and stillness, or proposed it modestly. You should not have sought to exhaust the Royal treasury, nor haughtily despised all other philosophers, especially those upon whose principles you yourself rely. You are a most ungrateful student, running away from school before even paying your tuition <note>original: "didactro." Refers to a teacher's fee.</note>, only to vomit insults and curses against your Masters! But tell me,

<summary>Johann Joachim Becher exposes the hypocrisy of the alchemist Franz Gassmann (Pantaleon), noting that he publicly attacks others for methods he secretly tries to sell to the King of England as his own. Becher accuses Gassmann of being a fraudulent "jackdaw" stealing the ideas of Philalethes and Alexander Seton.</summary>
<keywords>Johann Joachim Becher, Franz Gassmann, Pantaleon, Eirenaeus Philalethes, Alexander Seton, Regulus of Mars, Sophic Mercury, Alchemy, Fraud, King of England, Order of the Garter</keywords>

<meta>This page continues Johann Joachim Becher’s attack on "Pantaleon" (Franz Gassmann). Becher is arguing that Pantaleon’s alchemical theories are merely plagiarized and misunderstood versions of Alexander von Suchten’s work, and that Pantaleon is hypocritical for criticizing Eirenaeus Philalethes while using the same methods.</meta>

# Of Master Doctor Becher
<page-num>114</page-num>
<header>Of Master Doctor Becher</header>
these [critics], to say nothing of Solinus Salzthal <note>A pseudonym often associated with fraudulent alchemical claims or controversial figures of the period.</note>, [will ask] why Philalethes is unfaithful, why he mocks the Reader, and in what matter you have acted more faithfully and communicated more to the world than Philalethes. Do you not recognize that the Theory and Practice of Philalethes belongs to Alexander von Suchten? <note>Alexander von Suchten (c. 1520–1575) was a famous chemist and alchemist who wrote extensively on the medical uses of antimony.</note> I ask you now, tell us where Suchten and Philalethes agree, where they have instructed or deceived the Reader, and what you have taught better than either of them. I can scarcely believe you understand the state of the case—what Suchten and Philalethes intend, in what they agree or disagree, or whether they instruct or mock the Reader. Truly, I shall pull the cloud from your eyes, so that you may see and realize how much you err in Philosophy, and how poorly and arrogantly you have behaved in matters of morality.

1. Suchten commands that the first slag <note>original: "scoreas"—the dross or waste material left over from smelting metal.</note> be kept until you know their use, for he says a mystery is hidden in them, though he does not wish to speak of it. You, however—divine as you are—ask why he should command the second and third slags to be cast away. Meanwhile, Philalethes generally rejects the shell <note>original: "testam"—the outer casing or crucible residue.</note>, as if it were not relevant to the Philosophical Work. Nevertheless, both a Medicine and a certain "Particular" <term>Particular: A "Particular" transmutation refers to a process that changes only a small amount of base metal into gold or silver, rather than the universal transformation promised by the Philosopher's Stone.</term> lie hidden in those slags, concerning which I have many observations. For they are almost miraculous; nor is the iron that you demonstrate to be hidden in them common iron, but it "pours out gold" <note>original: "Aurifundit"—a term suggesting the material yields gold when processed.</note>, which is why many years ago
<sig>H</sig>
<meta>catchword: we (nos)</meta>

---

<page-num>115</page-num>
<header>Pantaleon Unmasked.</header>
Marcus Freund, that famous Astronomer of Nuremberg, once published a small treatise under the title: *A New Year’s Gift, or a Particular from the Martial Slags of Antimony* <note>original: "Strena novi anni, seu Particulare ex scoriis Martialibus Antimonii." Antimony reduced with iron (Mars) was a central focus of 17th-century alchemy.</note>. For these [slags] catch fire in the air; they contain within themselves "living Sulfur" <term>Sulphur vivum: "Living" or "native" sulfur, believed to be a more potent, essential form of the element in alchemy.</term>, which is amalgamated with Mercury. They produce a most beautiful "saffron" <note>original: "crocum"—a chemical powder or calcined metal, often red or orange.</note> and a deep red solution; they imitate Silver <note>original: "Lunam"</note> and the Mercury of Lead <note>original: "Mercurium Saturni"</note>, and they offer more present truth than the animation of the Sophic Mercury <term>Sophic Mercury: The "Philosophical Mercury," a refined, living substance seen as the essential starting point for the Great Work.</term> itself. You know now why Suchten commands the slag to be kept and Philalethes commands the shell to be rejected, but you are worthy of neither the kernel nor the shell; you lick the bark, worthy to lick the [blank space] <note>The double dashes in the original suggest a vulgarity or an intentional omission of an insult.</note> of the Philosophers, rather than to slander and accuse their truth. For how honestly Philalethes acted in his writings is well-attested—so much so that he must almost be forced into silence, lest you and those like you see the truth even against your will.

2. Suchten teaches how to make the Regulus of Mars from Antimony <term>Regulus of Mars: A metallic form of antimony produced by reducing antimony ore with iron (Mars). It was famous for the star-like crystal pattern that formed on its surface.</term>, with which Philalethes agrees. Suchten adds Silver <note>original: "Lunam"</note>; Philalethes does so as well, and he explained that his "Two Doves of Diana" <note>An alchemical allegory; Diana represents silver, and the doves represent the spirit or vaporized essence used in purification.</note> are nothing other than two parts of Silver in respect to the Regulus of Mars. The amalgamation, digestion, washing, sublimation, or distillation is the same as in Suchten’s work, so much so that Philalethes only imposed a figurative obscurity upon Suchten’s process—namely, he handed it down as a riddle.
<sig>H 2</sig>

<summary>Becher argues that the alchemists Alexander von Suchten and Eirenaeus Philalethes describe the same fundamental process using antimony and iron, despite their different allegorical language. He mocks Pantaleon for failing to understand the value hidden in the chemical "slags" and for accusing Philalethes of deceit when the process is grounded in established chemical practice.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Becher's critique of the alchemist "Pantaleon" (Franz Gassmann), specifically comparing his work to that of Alexander von Suchten and Eirenaeus Philalethes. Becher is exposing how "G" (Gassmann) claims originality for processes involving the "animation" of Mercury which were already detailed by these earlier masters.</meta>

# Of Master Doctor Becher
<page-num>116</page-num>
<header>Of Master Doctor Becher</header>
<meta>catchword: men-</meta>

[he] handed down, which *Suchten* <note>Alexander von Suchten (c. 1520–1575), a chemist who argued that all metals were derived from antimony.</note> publicly expressed with the clearest words a century ago. What do you say to this? Is *Suchten* therefore an impostor because he explains the same truth in other words in our time? Or will those who are to come and see after us be impostors because they saw the same Sun, though they used different eyeglasses? Furthermore, it must not be overlooked in this place that *Philalethes* <note>Eirenaeus Philalethes, a pseudonym for a famous 17th-century alchemist, often identified as George Starkey.</note> not only followed *Suchten*, but even progressed further where *Suchten* stopped. For concerning the repetition of the animation of **Mercury** with the **Regulus** <note>original: "Regulo" — the metallic form of a mineral; here specifically "Regulus of Mars," a fusion of antimony and iron.</note>, *Suchten* is silent, but *Philalethes* opens the Reader’s eyes through his **eagles** <note>Alchemical shorthand for the number of distillations or purifications performed on a substance.</note>. *Suchten* indeed says that **Mercury** animated through the **Regulus of Mars** has acquired the power of dissolving and separating metals—namely, the fire from **Mars**, which may act anew. And if, according to **G—** <note>Referring to Gassmann/Pantaleon.</note>, there is nothing from **Mars** in the **Regulus**, whence then comes that fire? Whence comes that power of separation and revivification of which *Suchten*, *Claveus* <note>Gaston "Claveus" Dulco, a defender of alchemy.</note>, and *Philalethes* speak? And why, as evidenced by his own letters from **G—** to the King of England, does he himself use such a **Regulus** and **Mercury**? Yet, regarding the fact that it must be animated by so many eagles, and that it must be rectified and sharpened over so many metals until it acquires the power of resolving gold, *Suchten* remained entirely silent—except that he speaks of **Venus** <note>The alchemical name for copper.</note>, and *Philalethes* highly recommends the **wedlock of Venus**.

---

<page-num>117</page-num>
<header>Pantaleon Unmasked.</header>
<sig>H 3</sig>
<meta>catchword: us</meta>

And this is the whole and sole secret that **G—** learned from his Priest, although the Author of the *Chemical Wedding*, *Christian Rosencreuz* <note>The legendary founder of the Rosicrucian Order.</note>, disapproves of **Venus**; if he does not call that way entirely Sophistical <note>Meaning deceptive or false alchemy.</note>, he certainly rejects it as a long path, and one which was enjoined upon him as a punishment. Otherwise, neither *Suchten* nor *Philalethes* would have written of the business so clearly that men would no longer even trade with him. Seeing that metals are somewhat altered and changed by the withdrawal of **Mercury**, hence the skirmishes and public exposures of **B-Z-C-W-W-B-C-W-** and others. I would certainly dare to say this: **Mercury**, however it is animated, if it casts down black dross, is not yet that homogeneous **Sophic Mercury** of which *Philalethes* writes in his *Experiments on Sophic Mercury*, which is turned by digestion into whiteness and **fixed Moon** <note>original: "Lunam fixam" — alchemical code for silver.</note>. Of this, *Lullius* <note>Ramon Lull, a medieval polymath to whom many alchemical works were falsely attributed.</note> speaks in his *Minera*, and I in my *Augmented Sand-Ore* <note>original: "Minera mea arenaria aucta" — one of Becher's own chemical treatises.</note>. For the blackness of **Mercury**, while it is very good, is nevertheless a sign of the reaction and analysis of the earthy parts; this blackness is indeed required in every way for the union with **Gold**, but we are speaking of **Sophic Mercury**. Thus, I infer that **Mercury** animated through **Venus** will never become **Moon** <note>Silver</note> by digestion alone; for the **Lunar Arsenic** <note>A specific alchemical preparation of arsenic and silver.</note> by which **Mercury**...

<summary>Becher continues his attack on Gassmann (Pantaleon), arguing that his alchemical "secrets" regarding the animation of Mercury are merely derivative of Alexander von Suchten and Philalethes. He disputes the technical validity of Gassmann's "Venus" path, comparing it to the theories of Christian Rosencreuz and his own works on mineralogy.</summary>
