<meta>This page continues Master Doctor Becher’s argument regarding the "animation" of Mercury. He is explaining that while earlier alchemists like Suchten and Philalethes hinted at the role of arsenic in this process, they remained vague. Becher intends to clarify how certain minerals, like bismuth, provide the necessary "arsenical" qualities to transform Mercury into a state capable of transmuted metals.</meta>

# Master Doctor Becher
<page-num>118</page-num>
<header>Master Doctor Becher</header>

[mercury] must be animated, a fact which is completely passed over in silence by **Lully** <note>Ramon Llull (c. 1232–1315), a major figure in medieval alchemy.</note>, **Suchten**, and **Philalethes**. Without that **Arsenical Fate** <note>original: "Sorte"; likely a typo for "Sulphure" (Sulfur) or a play on words.</note> or **Arsenical Sulfur**, **Mercury** is not "fixed" <note>rendered non-volatile or solid.</note>. Yet with that **Arsenic**, the **Mercury** that is found within the **Regulus of Antimony** <note>original: "Regulo Antimonii" — the metallic heart or refined portion of antimony.</note> can be transmuted into all **Metals**, as can even common **Mercury**, as **Suchten** and **Philalethes** teach—a point which anyone who wishes can testify to. However, concerning which metals **Mercury** acquires this **Arsenical quality** from—so that by a single extraction over **Silver** <note>original: "Luna" (the Moon), the alchemical name for silver.</note> it renders it fixed—both **Suchten** and **Philalethes** have remained silent. The latter, however, does slightly point toward the management of the paths, the **Planets**, and the **Mercurial Chaos**, but so faintly that it can hardly be noticed. For just as in **Magnesia** or **Bismuth**, there is an **Arsenical Salt** or **Sulfur** which, when mixed with **Tin** or **Lead**, renders them extremely thin (so that they may even pass through leather in an **amalgamation** <note>the process of mixing mercury with another metal.</note>), and in common distillation impresses notable qualities upon the **Mercury**, so too there is a certain **Mineral** that communicates **Arsenic** to **Mercury**. From this, the **Mercury** takes on an entirely different nature than if it were treated with common **Sulfur**. For it is not common **Sulfur** nor common **Mercury**, but the **Arsenical** principles of **Sulfur** and **Mercury** that are the true origins of metals. This I can demonstrate to the eye through **manual operation** <note>original: "Enchirisi" from the Greek "encheirēsis"; a practical laboratory demonstration.</note>, and perhaps no one before me has noted these things so clearly. They shall animate their **Mercury**...

<meta>catchword: cury</meta>

---

<page-num>119</page-num>
<header>Pantaleon Unmasked</header>

...cury forever in vain, those who do not imprint **Arsenical qualities** upon it through their "animations"—that is, to sharpen it and animate it with that **resolving Salt**. For **Mercury** is a fluid **Arsenic**. 

Listen now to what you, in your ignorance, have prophesied concerning the **Saltpeter of the Philosophers** <note>original: "Nitro Philosophorum".</note>, and learn how unfairly you attack **Solinus Salzthal** <note>A pseudonym for an alchemist, often associated with a 1624 work titled "The Triumphal Chariot of Antimony."</note> on his account. Learn what the **Magic Antimony** of **Suchten** truly is, and the true **Arsenic** of **Philalethes** in his experiments on **Sophic Mercury** <note>the "Philosophical Mercury" or refined mercury of the alchemists.</note>. Learn also what constitutes the **Triumphal Arches** of **Solinus Salzthal**'s mineral springs. Then, if you still wish to be a scholar, you might be able to offer realities to Kings, for all other things are vanity.

3. **Suchten** mentions a certain **Gold** being made from his **Mercury**, which he calls **Sophistic** <note>artificial or false.</note> because, in amalgamation, it was turned back into **Mercury** with other **Mercury**. **Philalethes**, however, calls this Gold the true **Physical Gold**, which is highly necessary and profitable for the **Art** <note>the Great Work of alchemy.</note>. He claims its special proof is that it is turned into Mercury through a light **Amalgamation**, which wonderfully accelerates the work. See, therefore, how envious **Suchten** was in deterring the Reader from this **Gold**, and how noble **Philalethes** was in noting and commending it. And see what kind of man you were, who wished to extract that **Gold** from **Iron** <note>original: "Marte" (Mars), the alchemical name for iron.</note> by means of **Royal Water** <note>original: "Aquam Regis" — a mixture of nitric and hydrochloric acids.</note>. Although you might perhaps extract it from the dross, **Philalethes**...

<sig>H 4</sig>
<meta>catchword: the-</meta>

<summary>Becher critiques Pantaleon (Franz Gassmann) for failing to understand the role of "Arsenical" qualities in animating Mercury, a secret Becher claims to have mastered. He contrasts the views of Suchten and Philalethes on "Sophistic Gold," arguing that what Suchten dismissed as false is actually the essential Physical Gold of the alchemical process.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Master Doctor Becher’s critique of earlier alchemists. He is contrasting the secretive and discouraging writings of Alexander von Suchten (Suchtenius) with the more encouraging, though still veiled, testimony of Eirenaeus Philalethes regarding the extraction of "Mercury" from metals like iron (Mars) to create the Philosopher's Stone.</meta>

# Master Doctor Becher
<page-num>120</page-num>
<header>Of Master Doctor Becher</header>
<meta>Catchword: Tin-</meta>

...yet he [Suchtenius] nonetheless commands one to reject the shell <note>original: "testam"; the crust or dross formed during smelting.</note> in the **Regulus** <note>the metallic heart of an ore</note>, which although it might be entirely Gold, is not *our* Gold; namely, it is our red **Arsenic**, the dyeing agent, or **Arsenical sulfur**, or **Mercurial Arsenic**. *Suchtenius* nonetheless mentions this when he says: it once happened to him that the best **Silver** <note>original: "Luna optima"</note> was tinted with a Golden color, but that this operation fails unless it has a good Chemist.

4. *Suchtenius*, being entirely envious and untrustworthy, not only leads the reader away from **physical Gold**—which he declares to be **Sophistical** <note>false or deceptive gold created by trickery</note>—but he even wishes the reader to do without that small amount of common gold elicited by the help of this path. He says it is done without profit, and yields only as much Gold as there was **Mercury of Mars** <note>original: "Mercurii Martis"; a metallic essence extracted from iron.</note>. Here he remained completely silent about the fact that at least half an ounce of the **Mercury of Mars** is elicited from every pound of **Mars** [iron], and concerning what he says of the remaining **Mercuries** of the bodies—that they have no form of Gold—he is silent there about their **Arsenic**, which is separated in the black powder. Yet here, in the case of **Mars**, he calls it the **Mercury of Mars**. *Philalethes* <note>Eirenaeus Philalethes, a famous 17th-century alchemist often identified as George Starkey.</note>, on the contrary, frankly admits that he produced true Gold and Silver from known Matter, and that all **Mercury** of the bodies contributes much more to the work and its **augmentation** <note>the process of increasing the power or quantity of the alchemical medicine</note> than common **Mercury**. Finally, *Suchtenius* admits toward the end that no

---

<page-num>121</page-num>
<header>Pantaleon Unmasked.</header>
<sig>H 5</sig>
<meta>Catchword: or</meta>

**Tincture** <note>the elixir or powder used for transmutation</note> can be acquired unless the **"Gold-nature"** <note>original: "Aureitas"</note> is introduced into the **Mercury**, and that this **Gold-nature** exists only in **Martial Gold** <note>Gold associated with iron or the "Mercury of Mars."</note>. However, he claims they all died before attaining the art, which he then dissuades the reader from pursuing. *Philalethes*, on the contrary, publicly testifies that he not only made it from **Physical Gold** but also from common bodily gold, and that he survived both the making and the result—indeed, that he is still among the living. He exhorts all who are called to this art, commending and defending the art itself as possible and most useful to the human race. See, then, how that Englishman *Philalethes* was more candid than our German *Alexander Sedonius* <note>Alexander Seton (d. 1604), a famous wandering alchemist known as "The Cosmopolite."</note>. You might object that perhaps he did not possess the art to that extent; but read what *Toxites* <note>Michael Toxites, an editor of Paracelsus.</note> writes in his preface to him, and what *Daniel Sennert* <note>A famous physician and chemist (1572–1637) who sought to reconcile alchemy with traditional medicine.</note> writes in Book 5 of *Natural Science*, chapter 1, concerning *Alexander Sedonius*. He records the proofs of **Projection** <note>the final step of transmutation where the alchemical powder is cast upon base metal.</note> he performed in **Cologne**, **Basel**, and other places. If, however, this **Sedonius** is one and the same as our *Suchtenius*—whom *Toxites* affirms lived a long time and was a Prussian nobleman, though *Sennert* makes him a Scot—this matters little to us, except that our **G —** <note>A likely reference to a contemporary rival or critic of Becher.</note> has acted most wickedly and presumptuously in attacking *Philalethes*, whose footsteps he himself follows, and whose doctrine he has not advanced by even a hair’s breadth.

<summary>Becher compares the secretive Alexander von Suchten with the more open Philalethes, arguing that the essential "Mercury of Mars" and "Arsenical sulfur" are key to true transmutation. He defends the possibility of the art by citing historical witnesses like Toxites and Sennert who documented successful transmutations.</summary>
<keywords>Alexander von Suchten, Eirenaeus Philalethes, Alexander Seton, Michael Toxites, Daniel Sennert, Mercury of Mars, Martial Gold, Tincture, Projection, Arsenic</keywords>

--- Page 94 ---
<meta>This page continues Master Doctor Becher’s critique of a contemporary alchemist referred to as "G—" (likely Franz Gassmann, who wrote under the pseudonym Pantaleon). Becher is accusing him of hypocrisy and commercializing secrets that should be free or kept sacred.</meta>

<page-num>122</page-num>
<header>Master Doctor Becher’s Pantaleon Unmasked</header>

...or elucidated, he has rather called it into doubt; meanwhile, the art which Philalethes <note>Eirenaeus Philalethes, a famous 17th-century alchemist whose identity remained a mystery.</note> freely exposes to the world in his experiments on **Sophic Mercury** <term>original: Mercurii Sophici; the "Mercury of the Wise," a specialized substance used as the starting point for the Great Work, distinct from common quicksilver</term>, this man offers to sell to the King of England for fifty thousand pounds sterling, which he carries indeed to the Thames. Regarding these matters, I finally protest three points against G—.

1. If he wishes to reproach authors, let him convict them clearly, bring forward experiments, and illuminate Philosophy with greater light; let him write from his own brain, and not compile the labors of those very men whom he mocks and offers to sell to others.

2. If he possesses or should acquire the **Tincture** <term>original: Tincturam; the legendary substance or "Medicine" capable of transmuting base metals into gold</term>, I pray he does not prostitute it by selling it for a high price; for it is entirely unphilosophical to offer the Tincture to Kings, let alone to sell it.

3. If he is an **Adeptus** <term>original: Adeptus; one who has successfully completed the Great Work and mastered the secrets of alchemy</term>, let him keep the laws of the Adepts, and not heap insults upon other Adepts, or indeed the greatest Philosophers. For if he does not heed these three points, he will not be a "Thrice-Greatest" Monarch <note>original: "Trismegistus Monarcha," a reference to Hermes Trismegistus, the mythical founder of alchemy.</note>, but will be held by the whole world as a fool and a slanderer.

In the meantime, there is no reason for him to fear that someone else might wish to write under the name of *Pantaleon*, seeing as G— himself has prostituted that name more than the Venetian nobles act the part of **Pantaloons** <note>original: "Pantaleones"; a reference to the foolish, elderly character in the Italian *Commedia dell'arte*</note> in the Bacchanalia of Venice, prostituting themselves as jesters to the people under that name.
